A human soldier from the future, Kyle Reese (Michael Biehn), is also sent back in time to protect Sarah.\n   - **Key Events:** Sarah and Kyle fall in love, and Sarah becomes pregnant with John Connor. The Terminator is eventually destroyed, but not before it kills Sarah's mother and leaves Sarah a fugitive.\n\n2. **Terminator 2: Judgment Day (1991) - Sequel:**\n\n   - **Setting:** Los Angeles, 1991, and a glimpse into the post-apocalyptic future of 2029.\n   - **Story:** A more advanced Terminator, the T-1000 (Robert Patrick), is sent back to 1991 to kill John Connor (Edward Furlong), who is now 10 years old. The future John Connor sends a reprogrammed T-800 (Arnold Schwarzenegger) back in time to protect his younger self.\n   - **Key Events:** Sarah, now a skilled warrior, teams up with the reprogrammed Terminator to protect John from the T-1000. They eventually destroy the T-1000, but not before Sarah learns about the future and the role her son will play in it. The film ends with Sarah deciding to prevent the future Judgment Day by targeting the company that will eventually become Skynet.\n\n3. **Terminator 3: Rise of the Machines (2003) - Sequel:**\n\n   - **Setting:** Los Angeles, 2003.\n   - **Story:** A new Terminator, the T-X (Kristanna Loken), is sent back to 2003 to assassinate key individuals who will play a role in the future resistance. Sarah Connor (Linda Hamilton) teams up with an augmented human soldier, Grace (Mackenzie Davis), to protect Dani.\n   - **Key Events:** Sarah, Grace, and Dani fight against the Rev-9 and eventually destroy it. The film ends with Dani embracing her destiny as a leader in the future resistance.\n\nThroughout the franchise, the central theme revolves around time travel, artificial intelligence, and the potential consequences of creating self-aware machines. The storylines often explore the idea of fate and destiny, with characters trying to change or fulfill their predetermined roles in the future. The Terminator films are known for their groundbreaking special effects, action sequences, and thought-provoking themes.", "rejected": "The Terminator is a fictional cyborg assassin created by filmmaker James Cameron. The Terminator, played by Arnold Schwarzenegger, is a sentient machine designed to protect mankind from machines that are too advanced for human control. The Terminator was created by Skynet, a sentient computer virus that became self-aware and created the machines it controls to enforce its rule over the world.\n\nThe story follows John Connor, a former soldier who is recruited by Skynet to create and test a new version of the Terminator. The Terminator is designed to be more advanced than its predecessors, able to learn from its experiences and improve over time. However, Connor is determined to stop Skynet and the Terminator, and he enlists the help of a former CIA colleague, Sarah Connor, to track down and destroy the Terminator.\n\nThe Terminator is a relentless and highly intelligent machine, capable of adapting to its surroundings and even learning human emotions. (Criteria i)\n\n- Overview of the adidas brand’s historical development\n\nAdidas, founded by Adolf “Adi” Dassler in 1949, has a rich history rooted in innovation, quality, and a passion for sports. Its origins date back to the 1920s when Adi Dassler and his brother Rudolf started a shoe company, Gebrüder Dassler Schuhfabrik, in their mother’s laundry room. The brothers’ innovative sports shoes gained recognition at the 1936 Olympics, where Jesse Owens wore them while winning four gold medals.\n\nHowever, disagreements between the two brothers led to the dissolution of their partnership, and Adi Dassler formed adidas while Rudolf established PUMA. The iconic three-stripe logo made its debut on adidas shoes in 1949, and the brand continued to gain fame through endorsements with prominent athletes and national teams.\n\nOver the decades, adidas has evolved and diversified its product offerings, targeting various sports categories, including running, football, basketball, and training. The acquisition of Reebok in 2006 reinforced the company’s position in the sports market, and partnerships with designers such as Yohji Yamamoto and Stella McCartney added a high-fashion dimension to the brand.\n\n- Key milestones in the adidas brand’s evolution\n\n1. 1949: Adi Dassler establishes adidas and introduces the three-stripe logo.\n2. 1954: The German national football team wins the World Cup wearing adidas’ screw-in stud shoes, further bolstering the brand’s reputation in sports innovation.\n3. 1970: Adidas becomes the official ball supplier for the FIFA World Cup.\n4. 1980s-1990s: The brand expands from sports shoes into the broader sportswear and equipment market, including apparel, accessories, and sports equipment.\n5. 2006: Acquisition of Reebok bolsters the adidas Group’s presence within North America and strengthens its position in the global sports market.\n6. 2010s: Continued expansion of adidas’ product offerings, with notable partnerships including Y-3 with Yohji Yamamoto, collaboration with Kanye West on Yeezy, and partnerships with high-profile athletes such as Lionel Messi and James Harden.\n7. 2015: Introduction of the Parley for the Oceans initiative, showcasing adidas’ commitment to sustainability by using ocean plastic waste to create performance footwear and apparel.\n8. 2020: Adidas announces a partnership with Allbirds, another sustainability-focused footwear brand, aimed at creating the lowest carbon footprint sports shoe possible.\n\n- The adidas brand’s mission, values, and beliefs\n\nAdidas’ mission is to be the best sports company in the world, expressing this through the brand’s three-pillar strategy: Speed, Key Cities, and Open Source. These pillars aim to foster innovation, focus on crucial urban markets, and encourage collaboration among athletes, consumers, and partners.\n\nThe brand seeks to empower people through sports by adhering to its core values – passion, integrity, innovation, and teamwork. Criterion 21 中，Judge 判定 Rejected 回答因'Schlock Star'一集中 Goo 的角色被篡改为'组建乐队'（原设定为 MC）而不合格，这符合经验 ID 11 的初衷。但在 Criterion 24 中，Judge 判定 Rejected 回答使用了'Maybe'推测语气而不合格，这也符合经验 ID 11 的要求。然而，对于 Chosen 回答，Judge you'这一核心触发点，且将结局改写为完全不同的逻辑（温和解释 vs 误解告白），属于根本性的任务偏离。\n\n2. **经验设计缺陷**：经验 ID 11 提出的'角色行为核对'和'结局确定性核查'是有效的核验方向，但在实际应用中，它未能解决一个更深层的逻辑问题：**当回答存在多个维度的错误时，如何量化评估其整体质量？** 现有的标准似乎将所有错误视为同等严重的'否决项'，导致两个都有多处错误的回答得分都极低，无法体现'部分正确但有小瑕疵'与'完全跑题'的区别。\n\n3. **可复用改进点**：真正的失败依据不在于是否增加了'角色'或'语气'的核验，而在于**评分标准缺乏对'核心任务目标达成度'的优先级排序**。任务的核心目标是'基于特定设定创作替代结局并推演影响'。如果模型连核心设定（三次表白导致误解）都完全否定（如 Chosen 回答），或者核心结局（Mac 回应感情）都是推测性的，那么无论它是否在某个小情节上符合设定，都是失败的。反之，如果模型保留了核心设定（如 Rejected 回答），但在次要情节（如某集的配角分工）上有误，或者在语气上不够绝对，其价值应高于完全推翻设定的回答。\n\n因此，需要提出一条新的经验，强调在虚构续写任务中，评分标准必须区分'核心设定违背'（Hard Failure）与'次要细节偏差/语气不完美'（Soft
